class Solution:
    def secondGreaterElement(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
    ans = [-1] * len(nums)
    s, ss = [], []
    for i, x in enumerate(nums): 
        while ss and nums[ss[-1]] < x: ans[ss.pop()] = x
        buff = []
        while s and nums[s[-1]] < x: buff.append(s.pop())
        while buff: s.append(buff.pop())
            s.append(i)
    return ans

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

```
class Solution:
    def secondGreaterElement(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        ans = [-1] * len(nums)
        s = []  # indices waiting for first greater
        t = []  # indices waiting for second greater
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            while t and nums[t[-1]] < x:
                ans[t.pop()] = x
            tmp = []
            while s and nums[s[-1]] < x:
                tmp.append(s.pop())
            while tmp:
                s.append(tmp.pop())
            s.append(i)
        return ans
```

```
class Solution:
    def secondGreaterElement(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        ans = [-1] * len(nums)
        s1, s2 = [], []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            while s2 and nums[s2[-1]] < x:
                ans[s2.pop()] = x
            temp = []
            while s1 and nums[s1[-1]] < x:
                temp.append(s1.pop())
            while temp:
                s2.append(temp.pop())
            s1.append(i)
        return ans
```

Need output fixed code. But let's consider if there is an alternative interpretation: The buggy code might be for "next greater element II" with two stacks? Let's search memory of LeetCode 2454 "Second Greater Element" official solution:
```
class Solution:
    def secondGreaterElement(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        n = len(nums)
        ans = [-1] * n
        s = []
        ss = []
        for i in range(n):
            while ss and nums[ss[-1]] < nums[i]:
                ans[ss.pop()] = nums[i]
            tmp = []
            while s and nums[s[-1]] < nums[i]:
                tmp.append(s.pop())
            while tmp:
                ss.append(tmp.pop())
            s.append(i)
        return ans
```
Yes.

Need maybe fix indentation exactly:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def secondGreaterElement(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        ans = [-1] * len(nums)
        s, ss = [], []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            while ss and nums[ss[-1]] < x:
                ans[ss.pop()] = x
            buff = []
            while s and nums[s[-1]] < x:
                buff.append(s.pop())
            while buff:
                ss.append(buff.pop())
            s.append(i)
        return ans
```

Need ensure `s.append(i)` is inside for loop, not inside while buff. Yes.

Let's think if there is any subtle bug with using `buff` list and `while buff: ss.append(buff.pop())`. Suppose s has multiple elements popped. We want move them to ss in original order. We used buff to reverse. Let's prove with example s bottom->top [a,b,c] where c top. Pop all into buff: buff.append(c), append(b), append(a) => buff=[c,b,a]. Then while buff: ss.append(buff.pop()) => pop a, b, c => ss=[a,b,c]. Good. If only some popped, same for those. Good.

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def secondGreaterElement(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        ans = [-1] * len(nums)
        s, ss = [], []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            while ss and nums[ss[-1]] < x:
                ans[ss.pop()] = x
            buff = []
            while s and nums[s[-1]] < x:
                buff.append(s.pop())
            while buff:
                ss.append(buff.pop())
            s.append(i)
        return ans
```

Let's maybe search memory of LeetCode 2454 solution with variable names:
```
class Solution:
    def secondGreaterElement(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        ans = [-1] * len(nums)
        stack1, stack2 = [], []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            while stack2 and nums[stack2[-1]] < x:
                ans[stack2.pop()] = x
            tmp = []
            while stack1 and nums[stack1[-1]] < x:
                tmp.append(stack1.pop())
            while tmp:
                stack2.append(tmp.pop())
            stack1.append(i)
        return ans
```

Let's maybe decide final formatting. The final answer should be exactly:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def secondGreaterElement(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        ans = [-1] * len(nums)
        s, ss = [], []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            while ss and nums[ss[-1]] < x:
                ans[ss.pop()] = x
            buff = []
            while s and nums[s[-1]] < x:
                buff.append(s.pop())
            while buff:
                ss.append(buff.pop())
            s.append(i)
        return ans
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
should be only code. We can include `from typing import List`. Need not include reasoning. The final answer should be code. Let's ensure no accidental extra text.</think>```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def secondGreaterElement(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        ans = [-1] * len(nums)
        s, ss = [], []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            while ss and nums[ss[-1]] < x:
                ans[ss.pop()] = x
            buff = []
            while s and nums[s[-1]] < x:
                buff.append(s.pop())
            while buff:
                ss.append(buff.pop())
            s.append(i)
        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def secondGreaterElement(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        ans = [-1] * len(nums)
        s, ss = [], []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            while ss and nums[ss[-1]] < x:
                ans[ss.pop()] = x
            buff = []
            while s and nums[s[-1]] < x:
                buff.append(s.pop())
            while buff:
                ss.append(buff.pop())
            s.append(i)
        return ans
